ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e6916	We come to points 1 to 4 on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. Mr Bösch is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e6943	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President, I would like to thank you for setting the agenda as it has been set today. I am also surprised that Mr Zinggl is able to draw so little attention to international issues. This item on the agenda 1 seems to me to be a milestone in international cooperation between European countries. It is an agreement between Austria and the Swiss Confederation on cooperation in the field of cross-border air security against non-military threats from the air. According to the previous legal situation, Austrian pilots who accompany a suspicious civil aircraft must already reverse the border at the necessary distance and leave the suspicious aircraft, as they must not exceed the state border. This makes it possible for the suspicious aircraft to change its direction in the short term, or to return to Austria again, and no rapid resumption of surveillance is possible. The same applies also to Swiss pilots who have to turn around at the Austrian border. The new agreement, on the other hand, opens the possibility of crossing the common state border in order to be able to safely transfer a suspicious aircraft to the flight forces of the neighbouring country so that no surveillance gap arises. The use of arms on the other territory shall be excluded and cooperation shall take place in accordance with the sovereignty of the parties. Ladies and gentlemen, this seems to me to be a milestone. Here, a non-EU country with an EU country – and both are neutral, so the conditions cannot be more complicated – is an agreement to establish a follow-up in the context of airspace surveillance. I hope that this is only a first step on this difficult issue of airspace surveillance in Central and South-East Europe and that there will be further steps to ensure that we have a trustful cooperation. The Commission's work on this issue will be carried out by several European countries. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e7322	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear visitors to the gallery! I would particularly like to refer to the agreement which Mr Bösch has already mentioned at the outset between us and Switzerland. There is a parade example of how this can work well, which the Benelux states show us. The Netherlands, Belgium and Luxembourg jointly organised airspace monitoring in 2015. It is true that half of the Netherlands and the other half of Belgium will be taken over, Luxembourg does not contribute to any aircraft. This goes so far that this itself is involved in the procurement. Therefore, there are also analogies to Austria, where this could be the subject again, becoming a topic – we will see how our Eurofighters continue. This goes so far as even to direct the procurement. It is currently true that Belgium has F-16 aircraft from the 1980s; now they have only half of those they originally had. At that time it was almost a hundred pieces. Now the question of re-purchasing is raised. At the moment, at least according to media reports, it looks like it's going to change to an F-35 plane. One of the arguments – in addition to the cost-benefit arguments; it may also be that membership of NATO plays a role because it is manufactured in the USA – is also that the Netherlands also have these planes and of course it is much easier to work together when you have the same equipment. That is why, in this context, we also call on the Federal Government to continue to take further steps – also in the sense of the Presidency – for more pooling within the European Union in the field of airspace surveillance, but also in general, and, of course, to take further steps to focus on joint airspace monitoring. In this sense, I would ask the Federal Government not to end the borders at the Austrian border, but to go a step further in the field of aviation security and to think beyond. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e7586	Next speaker: Mr David Lasar. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e7610	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Perhaps a few words and details on the agreement between Austria and Switzerland on cross-border protection of airspace: The Agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Swiss Confederation regulates cooperation in the area of cross-border protection of the airspace against non-military threats from the air. What does that mean in detail and why is this agreement necessary? In principle, it is like this: If a civil aircraft is located in the Austrian or Swiss airspace and the Authority, the Austro Control, the radio contact to the aircraft is lost and cannot be restored quickly, the suspicion of the unlawful stay of the aircraft stands in the room. Military pilots must then be used to contact and accompany the suspicious aircraft. In this case, however, they must turn off from the border, as they were not allowed to enter the other airspace until now, so the Austrians did not enter the Swiss airspace and vice versa. This was not very useful, as this aircraft, which has not been precisely identified, is suddenly gone until the competent defense comes and may be able to take over this aircraft in the air. With the new agreement, the Austrians may also fly into the Swiss airspace and the Swiss into the Austrian airspace, i.e. one can then pass in the air the unidentified aircraft. I think this is for the safety of all, for Austria as well as Switzerland. We should also introduce this regulation with other countries, such as Germany, which has already been discussed in the spring of 2018. I think it is a very, very sensible treaty that we approve here today, and it would be important to conclude it with other countries. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e9690	Next item is the report by Mr Andreas Kollross. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e9714	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Dear visitors to the gallery, in particular – I am delighted as a Trumauer Mayor – the students of the HAK Baden! [[General condolences.]] And of course also value listeners at home in front of the screens! I would also like to make a few comments on the request for consistent action against the death penalty. I would like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur on his report. It may also be that we want to re-establish our efforts, and would like to add a few more facts about this. We live in a world where there are 106 states in which there is no death penalty. In seven states, the death penalty is only applied in the case of exceptional crimes, such as war crimes. Although 29 states still have the death penalty in law, they no longer exercise it. However, at least 56 states continue to apply and practice the death penalty. Thus, a third of all countries around the world are still designing their judicial system with the death penalty. In addition, one must unfortunately say that in this one third of the world, however, two thirds of humanity live at the same time. That is, the majority of the world's population, although only a third of the states are using the death penalty, is still threatened by the death penalty. That is why I believe it is right that, even because we are celebrating this 70 years of General Declaration on Human Rights and perhaps also in relation to the EU Presidency, we are intensively dealing with the death penalty and are really progressing here as a pioneer and insisting that we prefer a different kind of legislation. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] However, I also believe and would like to join with my colleagues, Mr Deer and Mr Noll, that a little bit of a shaky side-taste already has the whole thing, because you always feel that you are doing different reviews of the death penalty, so that death penalty is not the same as death penalty. ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e11412	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! With the environmental package, the National Council is dealing with four important legislative proposals for environmental protection in Austria today. In just a few months, we have prepared numerous demands from the European Commission for implementation, and my concern is to conclude positive EU infringement procedures with the entire environmental package and also to comply with international standards. The Aarhus Participation Act aims to ensure access to courts in matters relating to the environment of air, water and waste, in accordance with the Aarhus Convention. Since it has already been mentioned today: The different implementation results in Austria due to the different Skills. However, we are already in the process of preparing and implementing the relevant legislative projects in the federal states. Among other things, the novelle was a year-long demand from different organizations that we are now meeting. The draft law contains adjustments in the areas of competence of my department. It includes the Immission Protection Act – Air, Waste Management Act and Water Law, where we define participation and challenge rights, in particular for environmental organisations, in environmentally relevant procedures. This creates legal certainty for all parties involved, counteracts the infringement procedure opened in 2014, and is also in line with the European Court of Justice’s judgment in the Protect case. When IG-L exceeds Immission limit values, the competent national authorities shall draw up a programme of measures. The aim is to ensure that the maximum compliance with the Union's legal requirements is ensured. Today's novel of the Immission Protection Act – Air grants individuals and environmental organisations concerned the right to have the legal provisions relating to the preparation and revision of these programmes judicially checked. It is not a right to adopt certain individual measures. In particular, it is about the extent to which the set of measures as a whole is appropriate to ensure compliance with the limit values. I was particularly concerned that the Union's recognised discretion of national leaders should be preserved in the selection of the set of measures. Individuals or environmental organisations concerned may, within certain time limits, request a decision from the head of state in which the set of measures contained in the programme is to be determined. Similarly, in the event of the legal conditions, in particular in the case of limit values or time-limits, an application may be made for the preparation and revision of programmes. This decision is subject to review by the administrative courts. For the purposes of the Water Law Act, recognised environmental organisations shall obtain the right to participate and challenge in the context of water authorisation procedures. In the event of a significant impact on the water status of a project, they shall have an interest in the procedure and also a right of challenge of the decision. In the waste sector, the implementation of the Aarhus Convention affects the procedures of the ordinary authorisation procedure below the IPPC thresholds. Non-contained are ground-absorbing landfills. IPPC installations are already subject to public participation in the form of a party position. Seveso systems are equivalent to the IPPC systems. For the approval and substantial modification of waste treatment facilities below the IPPC threshold, a subsequent appeal to the Administrative Court is also foreseen for recognised environmental organisations. Finally, I would like to return briefly to the Immission Protection Act – Air. Together with Federal Minister Hofer, I recently brought in a package in the Council of Ministers to strengthen e-mobility. Transport is one of the most important areas of action of #mission 2030, our integrated climate and energy strategy. We know that above all there we are decisive our CO2 savings It is also important to ensure that the European The Federal Government is committed to a reduction of 7.2 million tonnes of CO2 by 2030. The package to strengthen e-mobility therefore includes concrete incentives to move towards clean mobility. In addition to numerous support measures for electric vehicles, this package is a very strong impulse to boost electric mobility in Austria. We are therefore keen on incentives and, with our projects, we want to convince people that it is paying off in several ways to switch to a clean vehicle. I am also particularly concerned that we are moving forward rapidly, and it is all the more gratifying that the course for the exception of IG-L speed limits for e-vehicles is already set in today's plenary. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] With this, we implement the first measure of our mobility package and can bring clean vehicles to the fore at the beginning of 2019. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e17898	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Over the past two decades, significant progress has been made in reducing emissions of air pollutants in Austria and, above all, in the European Union. Nevertheless, external air pollution is one of the environmental influences that, according to the WHO, cause the greatest health impact. With the Emissions Act 2018, a recast of the previous Emissions Maximum Air Act, we are implementing the new EU directive on the reduction of national emissions of certain air pollutants into national law. [[Abg. Preiner: Too late!]] In contrast to the previous NEC Directive, there are some innovations in the field of air pollution. Emission reduction obligations for the five main air pollutants sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides, volatile organic compounds, ammonia and also fine dust from 2020 and 2030, as well as a process for the creation of a national air content programme. On the speech by Mr Preiner: It is worth noting – and this has already been noted in the Committee – that Austria, particularly in the field of ammonia, exceeds the limit values, but also has something to do with the issue of animal welfare. Unlike in other countries, there has been a massive change in freemanship in Austria, which has been built. We are in the process of developing a national air re-content programme, in which, in particular, ammonia production will be a part. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Preiner: Too late, Mrs. Minister!]] . As Mr Preiner notes that this is too late, I would like to point out that in the past it was not especially possible with your party to implement it. [[Abg. Preiner: You haven't acted in your ministerial time until now!]] In the case of air pollutants, the air pollutant is also new PM2.5, which has not yet been covered by the NEC Directive. The emission reduction obligations are defined as relative values, i.e. as a percentage of the emissions of the base year 2005. In addition, the NEC Directive allows for the consideration of inventory changes and includes, in particular, requirements for monitoring the negative effects of air pollution on ecosystems. In accordance with the provisions of the Directive, the jurisdiction of the European Court of Justice and also of the Aarhus Convention, rules on public participation and access to courts for NGOs as well as for natural persons directly concerned shall also be laid down for the national air content programme. The new national air re-content programme, with measures aimed at achieving the emission reduction commitments, will be implemented by 1. April 2019 will be sent to the EU Commission and will make a valuable contribution to air pollution in Austria. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Preiner.]] I am very concerned to coordinate and shape the process of drawing up the national air content programme and also the work on the national energy and climate plan in close coordination. In particular, it is important to use synergies between climate protection and air pollution. Working groups on the sectors concerned, in which the federal states, the relevant ministries and experts are represented, will jointly develop proposals for action by the end of the year. After that, a six-week public consultation process is planned, as well as in the law. The technical scenarios are currently being revised and updated in order to have the best possible assessment of emissions development by 2030. With the new Emissions Act air, we take another important step towards reducing national emissions. – Thank you very much.

Point Report of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on the Government Template (272 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Federal Environmental Liability Act and the Environmental Information Act (281 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e18141	We're on our way to the 12. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Strasser is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e18168	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! This item on the agenda is about the novelle of the Federal Environmental Liability Act and the novelle of the Environmental Information Act. The second law, the Environmental Information Act, is not to be reconciled with the General Data Protection Regulation under the given framework, which is why a change is necessary. The first law, the Federal Environmental Liability Act, regulates the circumstance of the environmental complaint that can be brought in if, for example, an plant causes a water damage or a soil damage. This amendment is necessary, as the European Court of Justice and the European Commission have also asked us to draft this law in accordance with the law. In fact, this legislative innovation addresses the issues that the population and various institutions have to address in order to bring in an environmental complaint and what measures an authority has to take when an operator of an plant causes an environmental damage. So rules are laid down in this system. I would like to say that, in parallel to this legislation, the Austrian Water Law and the Waste Management Act are also of great importance, and ultimately, in many regions – for example, when it comes to water protection and the maintenance of small-water power plants – there are already recovery plans that the federal states and the district headquarters order; there are also certain rules to be observed. Finally, this legislative novel is a necessity that we are deciding today. We will endeavour to generate practical solutions in agreement with the institutions responsible. In this sense: Thank you to the government, thanks to Mrs Federal Minister, we are going in the right direction. - Good luck!

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e18359	Mrs Bundesminister spoke. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e18380	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The Federal Environmental Liability Act serves the prevention and, above all, the rehabilitation of significant environmental damage. The main substance of this amendment is the implementation of the EU Environmental Liability Directive in accordance with the directives and the adaptation to a judgment of the European Court of Justice. In addition, a infringement procedure by the European Commission has been pending since October 2017. The present amendment of the Federal Environmental Liability Act therefore allows for a directive-compliant adjustment of the definition of water damage on the one hand and the instrument of environmental complaint on the other. This will ensure that, according to the obligations laid down in the Environmental Liability Directive, contaminants of significant water damage have to take preventive measures in good time and also rehabilitating them. The environmental complaint also gives the right to call on the competent authority to take action. In addition, the Environmental Information Act is also being updated, where data protection terms are adapted to the General Data Protection Regulation. With the amendment of the Federal Environmental Liability Act, we ensure an effective instrument in the case of significant environmental damage. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e19344	Mrs. Köstinger has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-10-25-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00045_d8e19365	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! Part of the environmental package discussed by the National Council today is the innovation of the Environmental Impact Assessment Act, which was prepared, among other things, by virtue of the EIA Amendment Directive. The aim of the amendment is to implement the 2014 EIA amendment directive and several points negotiated in the government programme to increase procedural efficiency. As a result of the EIA Amendment Directive, the individual case-by-case examination to determine the EIA obligations and the criteria for the decision will be made more transparent. The Directive also calls for the future inclusion in the EIA of accident and disaster risks, climate change protection and, above all, climate change impacts as well as the progressive use of land where this is relevant. But above all, we are also implementing points which are enshrined in the government programme, including a system of competence for identification procedures for projects that go beyond the borders of the federal states. Presenting new facts and evidence is possible until the oral proceedings at the latest – this too is new. A faster effect of the conclusion of the investigation procedure, as well as the possibility of the establishment of a local attorney who can exercise public interests in the implementation of a project in the EIA process, will also be implemented. Here is the crucial word: can – if no location attorney is required, then this function is absent. The possibility of prior procedures should also be used more frequently to prepare the EIA-related issues at an early stage. It is important for both the project promoters and the authority as well as for the public that unnecessary documents are avoided and that there is a focus on the essential environmental impact. In addition, adjustments are made to individual EIA stocks on the basis of decisions of the European Court of Justice and also on the basis of facilitation for implementation. These measures enable us to make the EIA procedures more efficient and faster, while maintaining environmental standards. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] Once again, I should like to point out that people and citizens' initiatives relating to a procedure automatically have a party position in the EIA procedure. On the current debate on the amendment tabled and also adopted by the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, I should like to say that: That an organisation should demonstrate its own legitimacy by a minimum number of members was the wish of Parliament and the representatives of the government parties. I think it is also very positive to mention that there have been discussions between the environmental speakers of the government parties and NGOs. I think it is important, above all, that both sides have also used the opportunity for this debate. A recommendation of the opinion of the NGOs, which is implemented by today's amendment, was tabled, namely that names and personal data should not at any time be owned by the authority and that environmental organisations are also open to other ways of proving the criterion of membership. This can also be done, for example, by a notary, an auditor or lawyer, or by other means by the authority. At this point, I can really ask that the debate be a little clumsy! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] Let us not forget that NGOs in environmental procedures in Austria have never been granted as far-reaching rights as is now the case with this package of laws. The environmental package is therefore a major step forward in terms of participation rights. After more than ten years, we have implemented access to environmental courts in the fields of air, water and waste according to the Aarhus Convention. This was also a year-long demand of environmental organisations. I should also like to point out that Austria has the highest environmental standards in Europe. We spend around 700 million euros per year on environmental and climate protection.
